# CP2077 browser engine port — current build status

Work folder: `D:\cp2077 port`.

UI fidelity requirement: retain the original game UI, Ink code and assets. Do not recreate menus or HUDs in HTML/React or substitute a geometry viewer for the game.

The first requested milestone is an interactive original-menu session running natively in the browser that the user can record for Twitter. This prioritizes original menu startup while retaining the full-game goal. No menu has rendered or become recordable yet.

The UTF-16 compilation blocker is cleared. 98 selected module libraries and 8/8 selected startup units compile to WebAssembly. The full game still does not link or launch in a browser.

The original entry point, launcher and game state loop compile. Original initialization, running, shutdown, engine registration and low-level initialization units compile: 8/8 selected startup units.

The compiled libraries live under `build/lib`. Native platform implementation files are explicitly excluded and recorded in each module's result.json. A complete portable source selection does not establish that every platform service needed by that module works in a browser.

## UTF-16 changes

The game expects two-byte UniChar/wchar_t, whereas the Emscripten C library uses four-byte wchar_t. Forcing short wchar without replacing wide buffer operations would produce incompatible memory accesses.

All game compilation now uses -fshort-wchar and preserves the original two-byte assertions. `src/utf16-runtime.cpp` implements prefixed two-byte string operations, UTF-8/UTF-16 conversion, bounded wide formatting and UTF-8 file output. Engine CRT and locale conversion calls are redirected to those functions. Standard libc still handles narrow text. Tool generators keep their standard wchar ABI.

The selected link inputs contain 0 unresolved direct calls to the incompatible wide C functions checked by the build. This is a source/import boundary check, not runtime validation. The adapter does not implement every CRT locale/stateful encoding behavior or every formatting extension. UTF-8 is the browser conversion encoding; numeric parsing is ASCII. Unsupported formatting fails explicitly. Other modules require the same boundary audit as they are added.

## Missing generated code and settings

Built the original RTTIGenerator from its source into a Node-hosted Wasm tool. It reads 77 selected original engine compilation units and generates 21 type registrations, reporting zero errors and zero warnings. The generated header is mapped into the original expected include path through clang-vfs.json. It covers the selected engine sources, not every game module.

The shader headers existed in the archive but the original relative paths expected a different depot layout. The filesystem overlay maps those includes to the existing Mainline/engine/shaders files. This fixes C++ shader-header lookup. The original prepareHudTiles, dilateHudTiles and hud compute shaders now convert to validated WGSL through the pinned DXC/Naga pipeline in compile-ui-shaders.py. Native and final WGSL uniform offsets are reflected into per-scalar repacking maps, including matrix layout differences and required struct tail padding; shader include files and tool/output hashes are recorded. Runtime repacking, shader-cache identity, WebGPU module/pipeline creation and use by the original renderer remain unfinished. Four converted shader variants do not establish browser shader execution or full shader parity. The WebGPU shader/pipeline bridge now compiles and links with the retained platform bridge. Four compute variants from three original files are packaged, including both original DIRECTION=0/1 dilateHudTiles variants selected from staticShaders.xml. Runtime registry names and shader hashes are retained. CreateConvertedComputePipeline uses actual WGSL module creation and asynchronous WebGPU pipeline creation, waits for three validation/allocation scopes, retains the device through callbacks and rejects device replacement. No browser shader execution is verified. The original shader API adapter now compiles and participates in the strict game link: CreateShader, ShaderRef AddRef/Release, UnlinkBinary, compute SetShader and compute PrecachePSO. CreateShader accepts only the exact CPWGSL01 converted bytecode record containing WGSL hash and original runtime variant identity; it never chooses a shader from a debug name or accepts legacy DXIL. Creation waits for the actual compute pipeline. Bound command state acquires an engine ShaderRef and releases it on replacement/submission/shutdown; unsupported graphics stages poison the command list. Four converted .bin records are generated for later original static-cache integration. Wasm64 identity validation passed exact-variant lookup and stale-hash, altered-variant, truncation, trailing-data, null and legacy-bytecode rejection. This is CPU record validation, not browser GPU execution. The original shader-cache loader is not yet wired to these records. Original SetComputeShaderConstsRaw now copies caller bytes into command-owned compute state. Zero/null input preserves the original API behavior; copy-only command lists and blocks over 64 KiB fail. Original compute SetSamplerState now acquires actual sampler handles and retains engine references in the sixteen original sampler slots, releasing them on replacement/submission/shutdown. Unknown references, invalid slots and unsupported stages poison the bound command list. Both implementations compile and are present in the strict game link. These are pending command bindings: no WebGPU bind groups or dispatch consume the stored state yet. No shader execution or native menus are verified. The compute backend now creates real WebGPU bind groups from the selected pipeline layout and encodes actual ended compute passes with setPipeline/setBindGroup/dispatchWorkgroups. Creation and encoding wait for validation, OOM and internal error scopes; actual device limits are checked, duplicate group indices are rejected, and retained device identity is verified before completion. RecordGpuComputeDispatch connects this encoder to the bound original command list, retaining shader references and taking ownership of successful bind-group handles until submission/shutdown. Failed encoding poisons the command list. The bridge compiles and separately links against the pinned SDK. Original texture/buffer bindings do not yet build these bind groups; original DispatchCompute is not wired to this helper, and stored raw constants are not consumed by dispatch yet. Browser GPU execution and original menus remain unverified. The converted shaders also use read/write storage textures; their WGSL language-feature availability must be checked in the real browser before pipeline validation. Original compute BindTextures, BindTextureUAVs and BindTextureMipLevelUAV now compile and participate in the strict link. Sampled bindings use actual typed texture views; writable views select one real mip and the original 2D/array/3D layers, with cube storage using its 2D face array. Batches acquire real views and original texture references before atomic command-state installation, roll back on failure, and release replaced or retired pairs. Null arrays clear the requested original slots. Invalid usage, mip/range, multisampled UAVs and unsupported stages fail explicitly. These resource slots still need reflected bind-group assembly and original DispatchCompute integration. No actual browser texture binding, GPU execution or original menus have been verified. Shader storage format/access specialization and actual browser format capabilities must preserve original R8 normalized/clamped writes; silently substituting an R32 texture would change behavior. Shader-cache inspection confirms that original metadata and entry hashes are loaded before CRenderShader creation; converted bytecode files alone do not supply a complete static cache. That loader integration remains unfinished. Bind groups, shader parameter/resource binding, actual dispatch/draw and original menu startup remain unfinished. The module source selector now includes original .cxx source files. Compiling these wrappers exposes their real remaining GPU/platform dependencies; it does not establish rendering. Runtime uniform repacking now compiles into the WebGPU bridge and strict game link. Generated maps preserve original scalar bit patterns, transpose memory coordinates as required by the actual WGSL matrix layout, and include the full padded uniform span (HUD COM: 336 bytes). CreateGpuUniformBuffer connects the repacker to real mapped-at-creation WebGPU allocation. The reused Linux CSystemIO::CreatePath tried to mkdir an empty prefix for absolute paths and rejected existing directories. None of these compilation results establish browser save/runtime behavior.

The authored persistence-web.cpp bridges a live engine pthread to the browser main runtime using the SDK's asynchronous proxy context. The worker waits until the IndexedDB synchronization Promise finishes; the browser event loop remains free to process it. Storage initialization failure, write errors and commit failures report service errors. Calling the blocking bridge on the browser main runtime thread fails explicitly. Original save/delete/screenshot mutation completion sites use the bridge; screenshot buffers are flushed before the storage snapshot. Save serialization and metadata writes are checked before acknowledging success, and save identity is populated even if an optional screenshot cannot be opened. The filesystem adapter serializes automatic and explicit IDBFS operations so an older background snapshot cannot commit after a newer acknowledged mutation.

The persistence object separately links to the SDK browser runtime with its real Wasm export and JavaScript callback; generated JavaScript passes syntax checking. build/storage-bridge.js is dependency linkage evidence, not the game or a substitute UI. No browser execution, IndexedDB reload check, game-save operation or durable-save runtime parity has been demonstrated. Registration and settings/user-file persistence are now staged and compiled in the real service path; runtime validation is still required.

## Current launch blockers

## Physics dependency build

The original physics module compiles 72/72 units with 73 generated registrations. All 17 PhysX 3.4.2 components selected by the depot's Linux makefiles compile, totaling 455 original source units. Browser adaptations preserve 64-bit platform selection, full-width size_t hashing and the const transform getter. PVD temporary/metadata paths select browser filesystem locations. These changes are staged separately from the original depot.

NvCloth CPU and extension code compiles 18/18 units from NVIDIA's repository at commit 7bceea80d4a04649f495f4f4331d7e3bdcdb05ca. The depot has public headers and native binaries but no solver implementation sources. Twelve of thirteen public headers match the upstream code after comments/whitespace are removed; Callbacks.h differs only in static/dynamic linkage handling. Compilation uses the depot's original public headers and the same PhysX foundation headers as game physics. PxPreprocessor is force-included so every solver unit selects the same SIMD implementation. An initial link exposed inconsistent selection; after rebuilding all eighteen units, no PhysX/NvCloth symbols remained unresolved in the selected link inputs.

worldPhysics also compiles all 18 original selected units with 16 original generated registrations. These libraries are included only after every unit compiles. No physics simulation or cloth runtime parity has been verified; adding more game modules may expose further dependencies.

## Strict launcher link

The last strict link attempt included 98 libraries and has 974 distinct unresolved symbols across those inputs. It includes the completed Web font configuration rebuild, original ICU implementation/data, particles, shared gameServices, galaxy and telemetry. It also includes networkCore, replication and gameSave with the Web Galaxy registration enabled. Animation has now finished and is included in the latest strict link; current per-module states are shown above. Adding additional real engine objects exposes additional dependencies; this count is not the total number of changes required for the full game.

The original Premake executable and original usage resolver now generate the full cpLauncher project graph in a separate staged folder. Its configuration is Final/x64_Linux/gs=null; a browser project configuration and platform backends still require implementation. The original generated initialization source covers 107 modules and is compiled into moduleDependencies.o. Only its system-header include path was adapted. This resolves InitModuleDependencies itself while exposing missing individual module registrations. Source-file existence checks consult the unchanged original depot; generated outputs stay in the staged folder. No placeholder module initializers were added. Missing-symbol counts before and after including this complete initializer describe different input scopes and are not completion percentages.

Localization and shaderCache use their original source and generated RTTI. Browser cache filenames select the original PC asset naming convention; this does not make PC shader bytecode compatible with WebGPU. The compiler now includes the module root, matching the original include-directory list. filePathsWindows.cpp is included for its shared game/company-name definitions; its Windows implementation remains excluded by its original platform guard. Core follows the original Linux exclusions for named-pipe editor tools.

resourceMaterial compiles 31 selected units and resourceMesh now compiles 32, including the restored SpeedTree wind parameter unit. Their original type registrations are regenerated from the corrected portable source selections. Their libraries are now part of the strict launcher link, including original material, texture, multilayer and mesh resource code. This is engine resource support, not a separate mesh viewer; no scene loading or rendering has been demonstrated. FreeType includes its original portable src/base/ftdebug.c platform unit, bringing its build to 40 sources and resolving FT_Trace_Enable/Disable.

Input now compiles 16 original portable units plus the authored input-web.cpp browser implementation. The original RTTI generator includes InputSystemWeb in its 12 input registrations. CGameEngine::InitializeInput selects that type for Web instead of the Linux null-input type. gameFramework now compiles 66/66 after the Web service class was added, but the full game link still fails, so this is a compiled integration rather than a running input path. SDK keyboard/mouse/wheel/focus callbacks enqueue original BufferedInputEvent values under locks. Focus loss releases held keys. Cursor hide/show/force reasons follow the original Windows implementation, with real canvas cursor styling and gesture-driven pointer lock. Viewport identity comes from the eventual viewport window attachment. Callback/DOM handlers are removed during destruction.

Input parity remains unverified: gamepads, text/IME input, keyboard layout/modifier distinctions, high-DPI coordinates and wheel scaling still require work. Cursor warping and device backlight control are unavailable in this browser implementation and reported explicitly; keyboard/mouse devices have no rumble motors. The engine link now requests PROXY_TO_PTHREAD with four preloaded workers so blocking original initialization can run away from the browser main thread. It still fails on missing engine dependencies; no worker runtime or actual input behavior has been exercised in the browser.

The new browser memory backend uses actual aligned Wasm allocations with locked range tracking and logical commit ownership. Reservations are eagerly backed; decommit clears pages but does not shrink Wasm memory, and CPU page protection is unavailable. GPU allocation flags fail explicitly pending a graphics backend. Browser allocator budgets are 512 MiB slab, 4 GiB TLSF and 2 GiB large allocations under an 8 GiB link maximum; initial linear memory is 64 MiB after the animation archive exposed a 17,833,008-byte static-data requirement above the default 16 MiB. Runtime availability is unverified. Call stacks parse actual Wasm offsets when supplied by the browser, and profiling records explicitly mark unknown code size. This does not establish native virtual-memory or profiling parity.

The original reflection RTTI tool generated 136 registrations. Bison 3.8.2 and Flex 2.6.4 generated the original expression grammar and script lexer. The expression parser, separate RedLexer implementation and generated scanner are compiled. Pointer-width script operands preserve their original 64-bit serialized width across the Windows/Wasm integer type difference. Config compiles after a browser path selection and a const-member assignment correction in the bundled older RapidJSON. Original FreeType vendor sources also compile into their own library.

The engine, reflection and renderData libraries were fully rebuilt after the PC cooked-resource field layout was selected for browser texture and multilayer-mask blobs. The original memory-budget initialization is included as a startup dependency. System threading uses real browser pthread workers, but CPU affinity hints are unavailable and reported explicitly. File modification times are converted from POSIX seconds/nanoseconds to the original 100-nanosecond Windows epoch representation.

`serve-engine-assets.py` supplies original assets directly from the selected depot with HTTP byte ranges and browser isolation headers; its Python source compiles. It has not been connected to an engine browser filesystem or run as gameplay. The selected Mainline/archive directory contains only `_empty.txt` (3 bytes). Mainline/r6 contains 711,802 files (154,586,399,884 bytes), which does not establish a complete cooked runtime asset set. A matching asset build/cook is still required; the source checkout is not itself a ready-to-run game installation.

The InitializeServices compiler blocker is resolved through the reflected browser service class described above; no null-service success branch was substituted. Actual service startup and persistence remain unverified. Further dependencies include telemetry SDK implementation, audio, Oodle, video, graphics and the remaining game modules. Compiled animation, HTTP and physics implementations still need runtime integration and verification. A browser graphics backend and shader pipeline and filesystem/resource streaming are unfinished. No linked game Wasm exists and no 1:1 behavior is established.

## Rebuild and evidence

`build-browser.ps1` builds the original protocol and RTTI tools, the UTF-16 adapter, engine objects and selected modules before attempting a strict link. It currently ends with unresolved-engine errors. It does not launch the old mesh viewer.

Evidence stays in the work folder:

- `build/modules/*/result.json`: compilation outcomes and platform exclusions.
- `build/engine-startup/result.json`: startup compilation outcomes.
- `build/build-result.json`: actual link inputs and failed launch-build state.
- `build/link.log` and `build/unresolved-symbols.json`: remaining dependencies.
- `build/unsafe-wide-imports.txt`: incompatible direct wide C imports found in selected inputs.
- `build-tools/rtti/generation.log`: original generator's registration report.
- `source-manifest.json` and build-tool manifests: original/adapted source hashes.

Only compiler, generator and link work was performed. No scene previews, browser gameplay tests or video recording were performed.

## Oodle checksum reference - 2026-10-11

Parsed the original MSF7 PDB public and module symbol streams directly. Analysis files stay outside the browser port under D:/cp2077SourceCode/analysis/oodle. Debug PDB identifies oo2::LZQuantumHeader_ComputeCRC in newlz_vtable.obj at text offset 897072, RVA 0xdc030, length55. oo2::rrLZH_CRC_Block is RVA0xdc000, length34; it calls rrBigHash64_SIMD at RVA0x173350 (PDB text offset1516368, length732). Quantum CRC is low24 bits of that custom 64-bit hash, not a guessed standard CRC32.

Confirmed dynamically using Unicorn2.1.4 x64 emulation, mapping the original debug DLL PE sections but never calling DLL initialization, Windows loader, game startup or host-native code. work/oodle-hash-oracle.py obtains twelve original checksums at lengths0,1,2,15,16,47,48,49,383,384,385,4096. For every length, direct rrBigHash64_SIMD low24 agrees with the wrapper and buffer alignments0,1,7,15 agree. SHA256s and vectors saved in checksum-oracle-vectors.json. This proves the identified function relationship/reference outputs for those inputs, not complete decoder safety or game correctness.

No browser checksum implementation is integrated yet. Next: translate hash initialization/48-byte SIMD round/finalization and verify against this original oracle before replacing upstream Kraken_GetCrc TODO. The decoder padding, malformed-input bounds, callback/allocator/scratch contracts and real compressor ABI remain additional work. No Oodle missing entry counted as resolved. No full game link or browser retest.

## Recovered checksum implementation - 2026-10-11

Previous goal turn progressed by locating and CPU-emulating the original checksum routine. This continuation implements that actual algorithm in src/oodle-checksum-web.cpp and replaces upstream Kraken_GetCrc TODO with the real checksum call. No Oodle ABI entry is counted resolved yet; original baseline remains76 supplied/34 missing. Goal stays active.

The identified native hash seeds four lanes each with A=3735928559, B=544179635, C=1466479909, adds the low32 input length to A and subtracts it from C. Data uses big-endian32 words; each48-byte round mixes four independent lanes with rotations4,6,8,16,19,4. Short tails are padded locally to48 bytes with zero, avoiding any source overread. Scalar finalization folds the twelve lane words in groups ofthree and applies the original final rotations14,11,25,16,4,14,24. Output is full hash64; quantum checksum is low24. Original disassembly and derived analysis stay in D:/cp2077SourceCode/analysis/oodle, outside the browser port.

work/verify-oodle-checksum-wasm.py compares actual compiled MEMORY64/pthread/O2 Wasm hash64 AND quantum checksum against original DLL routines under x64 CPU emulation.32 deterministic randomized/edge-length inputs include0,48,384,262144-byte quantum boundary; four pointer alignments each produce128 successful comparisons. Full64 comparisons prevent relying on accidental low24 agreement. The oracle asserts it reaches the return sentinel rather than accepting an instruction-limit stop. Runtime test is only this codec component, not full game/browser retesting. Validation records source and DLL SHA256 plus flags in build/modules/ooz/checksum-validation/result.json; user-facing copy outputs/oodle-checksum-validation.json.

compile-ooz.py now compiles four units, including the actual checksum, builds libooz.a, records the explicit upstream modification and retains license. Metadata sets ChecksumWasmVerified only if validation passes and hash matches current source. OodleABIIntegrated and DecoderExecutionVerified remain false. The archive is still not selected for game linking. No global undefined-symbol suppression or success placeholders.

## Isolated compression worker - 2026-10-11

Previous goal turn made progress by implementing and validating actual native-compatible quantum checksums. This turn adds an actual compiled decoder worker, not a renderer/UI substitute. compile-isolated-oodle.py compiles the three actual ooz decoder source units plus validated original-compatible checksum into build/game/codec/oodle-decoder.mjs and its wasm. This codec module has private non-shared wasm32 memory; the original engine stays wasm64. SIMD/O2, 1MiB stack, 512MiB maximum memory. Original ooz accepts int-length offsets; request allocation limit is128MiB each. These are explicit resource bounds, not claims of arbitrary native-size parity. Component builds successfully; no browser execution or full game retest.

src/oodle-decoder-worker.mjs instantiates a fresh private decoder for each request. Copies the input, allocates private output plus64-byte permitted decoder padding and64-byte guard regions, invokes real Kraken_Decompress, checks exact result length, guards and unchanged compressed input. Only actual output bytes are returned. Decoder instance is discarded, avoiding reliance on possibly corrupted allocator metadata after a malformed stream. Wasm traps become real errors. The original header explicitly requires64-byte output padding; the worker provides it rather than handing an unpadded engine buffer to the decoder.

src/oodle-decoder-broker-web.js creates a dedicated module worker per request, copies engine data rather than sharing its memory, validates responses and exact byte lengths, terminates on completion/error, and imposes a15-second deadline for malformed/infinite-loop cases. postMessage exceptions also terminate via the actual failure path. The broker has not been added to the game link yet and there is no native OodleLZ_Decompress definition. Next work is native worker-to-main completion proxy, precise Oodle flags/callback/scratch/allocation contracts and validation on original engine compressed streams before counting the ABI as resolved. Browser execution of this new worker is unverified; a private memory boundary is not proof of complete native decoder behavior.

Original MallocAligned source reinspection found its pointer header write is guarded by if(x), correcting the earlier possible null-dereference suspicion for that helper. Kraken_Create/other allocation failure paths still need examination; no claim that upstream is fuzz-safe in engine memory. No CRC bypass, success stubs, native DLL launch, global symbol suppression or remade menus. Build status is build/modules/ooz/isolated-worker-result.json and log.

## Actual Bink allocator backing compiled - 2026-10-11

Previous goal turn was progress: compiled18 actual Bink lifecycle/async definitions. BinkSetMemory stores a validated allocator/free pair. CpWebBinkAllocateSdk invokes the original registered callback for real SDK backing allocations; -1 honors the original game's no-fallback failure signal, null permits real aligned default allocation, and each allocation preserves its matching release callback even if registration changes. CpWebBinkFreeSdk validates ownership and calls that matching free. Default allocation uses actual posix_memalign(32), not fabricated pointers. These exports are retained for the browser-main-thread allocator service and the allocator object is selected by future link-bootstrap.ps1.

Added bink-memory-client-web.mjs: private SDK worker uses a32-byte shared reply channel for synchronous allocator callbacks. Main broker calls actual native allocator/free exports, signals real replies with Atomics, and shares the actual engine WebAssembly.Memory object. Emscripten's own current pthread library passes wasmMemory to worker similarly (local primary evidence libpthread.js:436), so this is the actual shared-memory transport pattern. Guest addresses remain private; the real allocator's backing bytes are cached in guest CPU RAM and synchronized at SDK call boundaries. Initialization copies actual backing bytes into guest blocks; driver writes use write-through, before/after calls synchronize actual live buffers, and free publishes final bytes before invoking original free. The backing buffers contain SDK data and are used as storage, not unused allocations made only to trigger callbacks.

Guest virtual addresses and host backing pointers are deliberately distinct. Public movie facade and plane copying remain responsible for pointer translation; private allocator contents may contain guest virtual pointers and must not be presented as a directly executable native SDK heap. This doubles backing/cache memory and current whole-live-buffer synchronization is expensive, especially with large clips. Runtime ownership/coherence/performance across browser workers is not yet verified. Do not claim full native allocation/performance parity or successful game menus from compilation.

VM memory hooks remain optional for existing direct Node codec diagnostics, but the actual browser worker requires real shared engine backing and memory service. SDK callback OOM returns original -1 sentinel; driver allocation failures throw instead of using invalid pointers. Guest allocation failure releases the reserved host backing. SDK call shadows retain configured32-byte alignment in backed mode. Driver asset/framebuffer writes also update backing immediately so pre-call refresh cannot erase them. Worker and broker handle allocator failures as real failures.

Fatal cleanup requires an explicit terminal idle response: worker sets faulted before reporting a CPU/platform fatal error and rejects further operations; broker can then free all SDK backing with matching callbacks. On timeout/uncertain worker termination, backing is intentionally retained, since termination intent alone does not prove it stopped touching shared bytes. This avoids use-after-free. Normal movie/SDK close should release allocations via real callbacks; this new cross-worker path has not yet been executed, so zero-allocation claims from earlier Node tests do not prove it.
